The drawer cabinet is on the LEFT side of the agentview image. Note egocentric: +y = robot-LEFT = image-RIGHT. In agentview image, the cabinet is on the left of image. Wait, this agentview is from a camera looking at the table. Let's think: image left = ... Actually let's just use back_project.

The cabinet appears at image left (col ~0-300 in high-res 1024). Hmm, actually in the high-res image the cabinet (black with handles) is at columns 0-330, rows ~380-860. In the 256 policy image the cabinet is on the right. Interesting — the policy image seems flipped/mirrored relative to agentview_high? Look: policy image shows cabinet at right, stove at left, plate at bottom center, bowl center. agentview_high shows cabinet at LEFT, stove at RIGHT. So the policy image is rotated 180° or mirrored. Hmm, actually the policy image is the standard LIBERO agentview (rotated 180 degrees). Indeed LIBERO's agentview image is vertically flipped relative to... Let's not care; use agentview_high consistently with back_project (it's the calibration-frame image).

Wait, but actually, careful: the high image might be flipped relative to the low. The tool says high-res is the calibration frame used for pixel selection, and back_project default resolution high uses high world map. So use high consistently.

Also the wrist image is a near-vertical view: plate at top, bowl center, cream cheese left, wine bottle bottom center (the black round thing with cork = wine bottle from top), cabinet right with wooden slats.

Hmm, wrist view shows cabinet on the RIGHT. And the drawer handles are vertical bars on the left edge of the black cabinet in wrist view at right... Actually in wrist view, at right side there's a black block (cabinet) with vertical handles at top-right. And brown wooden door at bottom right.

Releasing is irrelevant to the check (confirmed: placing a\nmug in the correct caddy pocket fired while still grasped; placing/releasing in\nthree WRONG pockets never fired).\n**How to apply:**\n- To disambiguate among candidate destinations, carry the still-grasped object\n  (gripper +1) low over each candidate and read `libero_terminated` from the\n  returned state. Move to the next candidate if it stays false \u2014 no release, no\n  re-grasp, no commitment. This turns an N-way target guess into N cheap probes.\n- Only `release` after the flag is already true (or to seat a confirmed target).\n- Corollary: a non-firing predicate with a clean, low, upright placement is\n  evidence of WRONG DESTINATION, not wrong physics \u2014 switch targets first.\n- Caveat: some tasks pair In() with a \"not in gripper\"/stack-stability term; if a\n  held probe won't fire but the geometry looks perfect, release once to check.\n**Falsify:** a place task where the predicate provably requires the gripper open\n(object released) and a correctly-posed held object does NOT fire.\n**Related:** [[caddy-back-is-near-robot-pocket]]\n"

  "content": "---\nid: probe-container-floor-by-stall-height\nscope: global\nkind: perception\ntitle: Find a container's true floor by descend-until-OSC-stall and read the stall\n  height\napplies_when: you must place a held object on the floor of a container (basket/drawer/box)\n  whose interior floor you cannot see from above (occluded by rim/liner/other objects)\n  and depth/segment centroids are rim-biased\nsymptom:\n- descend into basket stalls high above floor\n- cannot tell floor from wall\n- object placed on rim not floor\n- second object lands on first\n- unsure if seated\nevidence:\n  cells:\n  - 10_swap_t0_s0\n  attempts: 4\n  solved_seeds:\n  - 0\nconfidence: single-shot\nrelated:\n- basket-two-cans-wedge-not-stack\n---\n\n\nDescend the held object with move_to (small step_clip ~0.02) and read the eef z where\nit stalls: a LOW stall = the object bottomed on the FLOOR (seated); a HIGH stall = it\nis resting on a wall/rim or on another object. Reposition xy and re-descend until the\nstall drops to the floor value.\n\n**Why:** move_to OSC servo stops making downward progress the instant the held object\ncontacts a rigid surface. The eef z at stall directly encodes the contact height, so\nthe same descend that seats an object also probes the geometry \u2014 a cheap, coordinate-free\nway to map an occluded interior floor pocket without trusting a segment centroid.\n\n**How to apply:**\n- Carry over a candidate interior xy, descend to a z below the expected floor with\n  step_clip 0.02; inspect final eef z and final_dist.\n- Establish the FLOOR value once (e.g. seating object #1): note its stall z (this run\n  ~0.542). Any later stall markedly higher (this run ~0.616-0.622, i.e. +0.07-0.08)\n  means wall/rim/on-object, NOT floor \u2014 do not release there; shift xy and retry.\n- The interior may extend OFF-FRAME (clipped at the image edge); probe a few xy to\n  bound the pocket. Re-derive per scene; never cache the absolute stall z.\n- Combine with the wrist cam for a sanity check, but the stall height is the reliable\n  signal when the wrist view is filled by the held object.\n\n**Falsify:** If a soft/compliant container lets the object sink so stall height does\nnot distinguish floor from wall, or if OSC stalls high even on the true floor (IK\nsingularity \u2014 then switch to move_pose), this heuristic breaks.\n\n**Related:** [[basket-two-cans-wedge-not-stack]]\n"

  "content": "---\nid: container-slide-axis-toward-fixture-body\nscope: global\nkind: strategy\ntitle: Close a drawer along the axis toward the cabinet body, and treat zero-motion-under-push\n  as a wrong-axis signal\napplies_when: closing (or opening) a drawer/sliding fixture whose position was swapped/relocated,\n  and a push that \"should\" close it moves the drawer by nothing\nsymptom:\n- drawer will not close\n- push does nothing\n- drawer floor edge unchanged after pushing\n- pi0_doubled close the drawer does nothing\n- container coordinates identical across many pushes\n- drawer never moves no matter the force\nevidence:\n  cells:\n  - 10_swap_t3_s0\n  attempts: 2\n  solved_seeds:\n  - 0\nconfidence: single-shot\nrelated:\n- probe-container-floor-by-stall-height\n- libero10-left-right-sign-verify-empirically\n---\n\n\nA drawer slides along the axis between its open position and the cabinet body it\nretracts into; to close it, push its open-side face TOWARD the cabinet body \u2014 do\nnot assume it closes toward the robot (-x). If a push moves the drawer exactly\n0 mm across several tries AND the trained contact skill (pi0_doubled) also does\nnothing, you are almost certainly pushing PERPENDICULAR to the rails; rotate the\npush ~90 deg.\n\n**Why:** A prismatic drawer joint only accepts force along its rail axis; a force\nperpendicular to the rails is fully reacted by the joint constraint, so the drawer\ndoes not translate and an OSC servo just stalls in contact (and the trained policy,\ngrounded on the base-task geometry, pushes the base axis which the swap invalidated).\nUnder `_swap` the cabinet is relocated, so the rail axis is whatever direction points\nfrom the open drawer to the cabinet box \u2014 read it off the scene, don't inherit it.\n\n**How to apply:**\n- Before pushing, locate the CABINET BODY in agentview. The drawer closes toward it.\n  Example (one seed): cabinet on image-left (-y) => close = push -y, even though the\n  drawer's floor looked like it extended toward +x.\n- Use a coordinate-free PROGRESS SIGNAL: back_project a fixed pixel on the drawer\n  floor/front before and after each push. If its world xy/z is IDENTICAL to ~4\n  decimals, the drawer did not move \u2014 change the axis, not the force.\n- Put the CLOSED gripper on the open-side face at face height (fingertips ~= eef_z,\n  so pick z just below the rim top), then move_to along the closing axis with\n  gripper=+1, step_clip 0.02-0.025, max_steps 120-150. One or two pushes seat it.\n- Near the cabinet, prefer move_pose over move_to (move_to walls at the OSC IK\n  singularity for forward/deep reaches).\n\n**Falsify:** A swapped drawer that DOES close under a push toward the robot (-x)\nwhile the cabinet is on the -y side, or a case where identical-container-coords +\npi0_doubled-fails still means \"push harder same axis\" rather than \"wrong axis\".\n\n**Related:** [[probe-container-floor-by-stall-height]] [[libero10-left-right-sign-verify-empirically]]\n"

If its world xy/z is IDENTICAL to ~4\\n  decimals, the drawer did not move \\u2014 change the axis, not the force.\\n- Put the CLOSED gripper on the open-side face at face height (fingertips ~= eef_z,\\n  so pick z just below the rim top), then move_to along the closing axis with\\n  gripper=+1, step_clip 0.02-0.025, max_steps 120-150.
